Based on the provided text, I will resume the outages identified in the text for Google Cloud Service HealthIncidentsBatch - Service Issues in us-central1.

Summary of Outage

The outage affected Google Cloud Services in the us-central1 region. The incident started on April 22, 2024, at 09:00 UTC and ended on April 22, 2024, at 22:18 UTC. The outage lasted for 13 hours and 18 minutes.

Outage Details

Here is the breakdown of the outage per cloud provider:

Google Cloud
Service : Google Batch
Region : us-central1
Availability Zone : (Not specified)
Impact Date : April 22, 2024, 09:00 UTC
Duration : 13 hours and 18 minutes
Root Cause : Increased transaction contention resulting in significant latency increase.
Downtime Period : 8 hours and 37 minutes
Resolved Date : April 22, 2024, 22:18 UTC

Output Table

| Cloud Provider | Service | Region | Availability Zone | Impact Date | Duration | Root Cause | Downtime Period | Resolved Date |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Google Cloud | Google Batch | us-central1 |  | April 22, 2024, 09:00 UTC | 13 hours and 18 minutes | Increased transaction contention resulting in significant latency increase. | 8 hours and 37 minutes | April 22, 2024, 22:18 UTC |

Overall Summary

The outage affected Google Cloud Services in the us-central1 region. The incident started on April 22, 2024, at 09:00 UTC and ended on April 22, 2024, at 22:18 UTC. The outage lasted for 13 hours and 18 minutes due to increased transaction contention resulting in significant latency increase. The downtime period was 8 hours and 37 minutes.

Note: I have removed references to cloud providers not mentioned in the text provided, normalized variables like regions and availability zones to standard lowercase official names of cloud vendors, and applied international standard name conventions for all variables of prompt response.

Here is the summary of the outage:

The outage started on September 4, 2024, at 06:29 UTC and lasted for 6 hours and 10 minutes. The root cause was a issue with VPC network data for newly created VMs or networks not being available when requests to Google APIs originated from these VMs/networks. The downtime period was not specified. The outage was resolved on September 4, 2024, at 11:06 UTC.

To solve this problem, we would need the following information:

1. The list of cloud providers mentioned in the text.
2. A detailed description of each outage, including:
	 Service impacted
	 Region and availability zone affected
	 Impact date and time
	 Duration of the outage (in days, hours, and minutes)
	 Root cause of the outage
	 Downtime period
	 Resolved date

Assuming we have this information, here's a general approach to solve the problem:

1. Create a table to store the outage data.
2. Loop through each cloud provider mentioned in the text.
3. For each cloud provider, loop through each service impacted and extract the relevant information (service name, region, availability zone, impact date, duration, root cause, downtime period, resolved date).
4. Normalize variables like regions and availability zones to standard lowercase official names of cloud providers.
5. Normalize dates to UTC or a consistent format.
6. Apply international standard name conventions for all variables.

| Cloud Provider | Service | Region | Availability Zone | Impact Date | Duration | Root Cause | Downtime Period | Resolved Date |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
